2. **Optimizers**:
   a. **GS-Fienup spectral** as before, but with improvements: Use a better target not exactly flat? Maybe target c flat over 2n-1 with endpoint adjusted? Since endpoints can be small, flat target forces endpoints=average, but optimum may have endpoints lower? Flat target equal average at endpoints may not be optimal; optimum c likely below average at extremes and above elsewhere? Since max just above average, to maintain sum, if many coeffs below average, max must more than average. If endpoints small maybe allows central flatten more? But max constraint? The sum average impossible. For min max, all coefficients should be ≤M; ideally many below with few at M. Endpoints can be arbitrarily small by setting a endpoints 0; doesn't hurt. So flat target with endpoints=average might force a too high at endpoints, causing score worse? However GS target ignored endpoint? It sets flat entire 2n-1, including endpoints; because DFT target phase update, a endpoints not directly constrained. But c_target flat at endpoints means it tries to optimize c0=average. Since c0=a0^2 can be small; trying to raise it might increase score? Maybe not if endpoints small not active. The GS target may be suboptimal.

Better target: Choose target c_k ≤ M with sum 1. The ideal target for min max given M is to put mass at 1/M? We can set target c_k = M for some set, and lower elsewhere. The lower bound for M is average. At optimum, many peaks at M, some lower. We can adapt target based on current c: For each k, target c_k = min(M_target, something) or cap. b. **LSE L-BFGS** as previous. Could tune beta, maxfun. We can implement more efficient LSE with cached FFT and use exact score. Need top-level. But previous already did; maybe need different initializations and more polishing.

c. **Multiplicative KKT/mirror updates**:
   - Use weights p_k = softmax(β (c_k - M))? Top active.
   - Compute b_i = Σ p_k a[k-i] via FFT or vectorized. Residual = b_i - M/n.
   - We want make b_i constant. For mirror update, gradient direction d_i = ∂(weighted c)/∂a_i - 2M/S? because stationarity. Weighted gradient = 2 b_i (if p active c normalized? Let's design helper functions:

```
def fast_conv(a):
    n = len(a)
    L=2*n-1
    M=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    return np.fft.irfft(A*A, M)[:L]
```
Using rfft faster for real. For spectral updates need complex fft of target length L padded to M. `np.fft.fft` target with M. `A` real? We'll use complex full.

`def exact_score(a): n=len(a); c=fast_conv(a); S=a.sum(); if S<... inf; return 2*n*c.max()/(S*S)

`def normalize(a): a=np.clip(a,0,1000); S=a.sum(); if S<... return; return a/S`

`def make_target_from_c(c, reduction):`
Input c nonnegative sum ~1 (or sum S^2? Use normalized sum1). Return target length L, cap C, or None. Need robust.
```
def design_peak_target(c, reduction):
    L=len(c)
    M = float(c.max())
    C = M * (1.0 - reduction)
    if C <= 0 or C >= M:
        return None, M, C
    t = np.minimum(c, C)
    deficit = float(1.0 - t.sum())
    if deficit <= 1e-15:
       return None,M,C
    capacity = C - t
    capsum = float(capacity.sum())
    if capsum <= 1e-15:
       return None,M,C
    add = deficit / capsum
    if add > 1.0:
       return None, M, C  # cannot cap this much
    target = t + add*capacity
    # due numerical, max may >C a bit; clip and renormalize?
    target = np.clip(target,0,C)
    ss = target.sum()
    if ss <=1e-15: return None...
    target = target/ss
    # if max slightly > C after normalize, maybe if ss<1? Hess. If target max > C*(1+1e-12), clip? But sum.
    return target, M, C
```
But if target normalized after clipping sum maybe not 1? We can avoid clip unless tiny: due float, target max = max under: t_i = c_i + add(C-c_i) = (1-add)c_i + add C ≤ C because c_i≤C. sum =1. Good no clip. For c_i==C, t=C. So max ≤C. Fine.

Spectral update function:
```
def spectral_update(a, target_c, eta, Mff=None):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    if Mff is None: Mff=1<<(L-1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.fft(a, Mff)
    C = A * A
    Ct = np.fft.fft(target_c, Mff)  # target padded zeros length Mff
    W = C + eta*(Ct - C)

    # we only take first n? Wait a length n, ifft full length Mff. The padding zeros in first n? For linear convolution, fft a length Mff; inverse of A gives a followed by zeros.     a_new = a_new[:n].real
    a_new = np.maximum(a_new, 0.0)
    S=a_new.sum()
    if S<... return None
    return a_new/S
```

Let's implement `spectral_peak_improve`:
```
def spectral_peak_improve(a0, max_time_s=300, initial_red=1e-3, min_red=1e-7, etas=(1.0,0.6,0.3,0.15,0.07), time_start=None, verbose=False):
    start=time.monotonic()
    a = normalize_pos(a0)
    if a is None: return a0
    n=len(a); Mff=fft_len(n)
    best=sc=a; best_sc=safe_score(a)
    reduction=initial_red
    stall_count=0
    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time_s:
        c=fast_conv(a)
        S=a.sum()
        if S <=0: break
        # Use normalized c? c sum=S^2. design target expects sum1. If a normalized sum1, S=1. But after normalize yes. Use c_norm=c/(S*S)
        c_norm=c/(S*S)
        target, M, C = design_capped_target(c_norm, reduction)
        if target is None:
            reduction *= 0.5
            if reduction < min_red: break/ event
            continue
        improved=False
        # maybe if reduction too aggressive often target None; if target valid, try etas
        for eta in etas:
             cand=spectral_step(a, target, eta, Mff)
             if cand is None: continue
             sc=safe_score(cand)
             if sc < best_sc - 1e-14:
                 a=cand; best_sc=sc; best_a=cand.copy(); improved=True
                 print...
                 if time... break eta loop
        if improved:
             # maybe slightly decrease reduction? keep; update target from new a next loop
             stall_count=0
             # optionally if M lowered significantly increase? keep
        else:
             stall_count +=1
             if stall_count>=3:
                 reduction *=0.5
                 stall_count=0
                 if reduction < min_red:
                     # try flat target spectral with tiny eta?
                     break
    return best_a
```

```
def lse_score_grad_scaled(a, beta):
    n=len(a); c=fast_conv(a); S=a.sum()
    c_norm = c/(S*S)  # if not normalized
    c_max = c_norm.max()
    scaled = beta*(c_norm - c_max)
    exps = np.exp(scaled)
    d = exps.sum()
    Lse = c_max + np.log(d)/beta
```

LSE gradient function:
```
def lse_obj_grad(x, beta, mu):
    a = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
    S = a.sum()
    if S < 1e-12: return 1e10, np.zeros_like(x)
    c = fast_conv(a)
    cmax = c.max()
    scaled = beta*(c - cmax)
    exps = np.exp(scaled)
    d = exps.sum()
    L = cmax + np.log(d)/beta
    P = exps/d
    # h = 2*b
    b = weighted_corr(P, a)  # P length L, a length n, using FFT
    n=len(a); h=2*b
    score = 2*n*L/(S*S)
    grad = (2*n)*(h/(S*S) - 2*L/(S**3))
    if mu != 0:
       grad += mu*(S-1.0)
    return score, grad
```
But `weighted_corr` uses FFT length 3n-2, O(n log n) as fast_conv. Good.

Let's flesh each function.

`fft_pow2(length)`:
```
def fft_pow2(length):
    if length <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(length)-1).bit_length()
```

`fast_conv(a)`:
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1; M=fft_pow2(L)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    return np.fft.irfft(A*A, M)[:L]
Could use `np.fft.rfft` with complex? Input real. Good.

`sequence_score(a)`:
```
def sequence_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, np.float64)
    n=a.size
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-100: return np.inf
    c=fast_conv(a)
    return float(2.0*n*float(c.max())/(S*S))
```
Clip? For internal, assume nonneg. Could clip: `a=np.maximum(a,0)`.

`clean_sequence(a, normalize=True)`:
```
def clean_sequence(a, normalize=True):
    a=np.asarray(a, np.float64).ravel()
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    S=a.sum()
    if normalize and S>1e-12:
        a=a/S
    return a
```
But if normalize True and sum too small return zeros. For score need >0. We'll use.

`capped_target(c, reduction)`: Need c sum maybe 1. If c not sum1, scale? Use c_norm.
```
def make_capped_target(c, reduction):
    c = np.asarray(c, np.float64)
    L=c.size
    cs = c.sum()
    if cs <=1e-15: return None, float('inf'), 0.0
    c = c/cs
    Mc = float(c.max())
    C = Mc*(1.0-reduction)
    if C <= 0 or C >= Mc or not np.isfinite(C): return None, Mc, C
    t = np.minimum(c, C)
    deficit = 1.0 - float(t.sum())
    if deficit <= 1e-14:
       # maybe no cap
       return None, Mc, C
    cap = C - t
    capsum = float(cap.sum())
    if capsum <= 1e-15: return None, Mc, C
    add = deficit/capsum
    if add >= 1.0:  # cannot fully cap
       return None, Mc, C
    target = t + add*cap
    # ensure fixed sum numerically
    ts = target.sum()
    if ts <=1e-15: return None, Mc, C
    target = target/ts
    # if max too close to C? ok
    return target, Mc, C
```
If add close to 1 but less, max=C; sum correction may exceed C slightly after renormalize if ts<1? But add computed to make sum1. If numerical ts=0.999999999, normalize increases max maybe ≤C*(1+1e-9). Accept. If target max > C+1e-12, we can reduce target to cap? Simpler ignore; small.

`spectral_refine(a0, time_budget, red_init, min_red, etas, verbose)`:
Need compute initial score. We'll use `t0=time.monotonic()`; deadline=t0+time_budget.
Pseudo:
```
def spectral_refine(a0, time_budget, reduce_init=2e-4, min_reduce=1e-8, etas=(0.8,0.5,0.3,0.15), verbose=False):
    a = clean_sequence(a0, normalize=True)
    if a.size==0 or a.sum()<1e-12: return a0
    t0=time.monotonic()
    deadline=t0+time_budget
    best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sequence_score(a)
    reduce=reduce_init
    stall=0
    eta_idx=0? 
    iteration=0
    print start
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if a.sum() < 1e-12: break
        c=fast_conv(a)
        S2=float(a.sum())**2
        c_norm = c/S2
        target, Mc, C = make_capped_target(c_norm, reduce)
        iteration +=1
        if target is None:
           reduce *= 0.5
           stall +=1
           if reduce < min_reduce:
               # fallback: use flat target?
               target = np.ones(c_norm.size)/(c_norm.size)
               # use step? maybe break or continue with flat
           else:
               continue
        if verbose and iteration%50==0: print(...)
        n=len(a); Mff=fft_pow2(2*n-1)
        # compute A, Ct once
        A=np.fft.fft(a, Mff)
        C=A*A
        Ct=np.fft.fft(target, Mff)
        accepted=False
        for eta in etas:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
            # Newton sqrt
            W = C + eta*(Ct - C)
            absA=np.abs(A)
            valid=absA>1e-30
            A_new = A.copy()
            A_new[valid] = 0.5 * (A[valid] + W[valid]/A[valid])
            # For invalid, maybe set A_new[~valid] = 0.5? keep A
            a_new = np.fft.ifft(A_new, Mff).real[:n]
            a_new = np.maximum(a_new, 0.0)
            S_new=float(a_new.sum())
            if S_new < 1e-12: continue
            a_new = a_new/S_new
            sc = sequence_score(a_new)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
                 a=a_new; best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc; accepted=True
                 if verbose: print
                 break
        if accepted:
            stall=0
            # maybe if improvement very tiny and reduce too high? keep; optionally slightly lower reduce if very small?
            # If target cap C achieved? Could compute new max; if new max <= C*(1+1e-4) and reduce big? keep
        else:
            stall +=1
            if stall >= max(3, len(etas)//2): # e.g. 3
                reduce *= 0.5
                stall=0
                if reduce < min_reduce:
                    # try flat target a few times?
                    break
    return best_a
```

`weighted_corr(P, a)` top-level:
```
def weighted_corr(P, a):
    n=a.size; L=P.size
    # P length should 2n-1
    Q = P[::-1]
    M = fft_pow2(n + L - 1)  # =3n-2
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    Qf = np.fft.rfft(Q, M)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(A*Qf, M)[:n+L-1]
    return conv[L-1::-1]
```
Using rfft for real. Q real if P real. P from softmax of c real. Good. Returns float length n. Need if P length mismatch? For safety pad/truncate? Use if L !=2n-1: return zeros? But P always.

`lse_objective(x, beta, mu)`:
Need returns tuple (value, grad). If with `jac=True`.
```
def lse_objective(x, beta, mu=0.0):
    a = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-12:
        return 1e10, np.zeros(n)
    c = fast_conv(a)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    scaled = beta * (c - cmax)
    # avoid overflow: scaled max=0, min may be large negative; exp underflows ok
    exps = np.exp(scaled)
    d = float(exps.sum())
    if d <= 0: ...
    L = cmax + np.log(d)/beta
    P = exps / d
    b = weighted_corr(P, a)
    h = 2.0 * b
    val = 2.0 * n * L / (S*S)
    if mu != 0.0:
        val += 0.5 * mu * (S - 1.0)**2
    grad = (2.0*n) * (h/(S*S) - 2.0*L/(S*S*S))
    if mu != 0:
        grad += mu * (S - 1.0)
    # For any zero a? derivative of c wrt a_i valid even if zero. Good.
    return val, grad
```

```
def make_initial_candidates(parent, n_base, rng, max_cands=6):
    cands=[]
    if parent is not None and parent.size>0:
        p=clean_sequence(parent, normalize=True)
        cands.append(p)
        # sym
        sym=0.5*(p+p[::-1]); if score better? add
        cands.append(sym)
        # perturb smooth
        x=np.arange(p.size)/p.size
        for amp in [0.02,0.05,0.1]:
           noise=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(p.size)) * lowmask)
           a=p + amp*noise; add if positive
    else:
       n=n_base
       x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       cands.append(np.ones(n)*0.5)
       # arcsine etc
    return cands
```

Main phases:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget_s-30
    parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None) -> clean if size>0
    base_n=parent.size if parent not None else 6962
    # candidate list maybe small
    best_a = parent or ones
    best_sc = sequence_score(best_a)
    # sym improvement
    sym=0.5*(best_a+rev); if sequence_score(sym)<best_sc: best_a=sym; best_sc=...
    print
    remaining = deadline - time
    # Phase 1 spectral on parent 50% of remaining
    best_a = spectral_refine(best_a, max_time=0.5*(budget_s-30), reduce_init=1e-3, ...)
    # maybe if time left, spectral on best again with lower red? or different etas
    # Phase 2 LSE LBFGS on best 20%
    for beta in [100,300,1000]: if time > ... run lbfgs; update best
    # Phase 3 apply spectral on best again 15%
    # Phase 4 perturbations 15%: for few perturbations run spectral limited.
    # Phase 5 larger n maybe 10%? Interpolate best to 2*? but time.
    # Final sym / exact eval
```

Upsampling function:
```
def resample_sequence(a, n):
    if len(a)==n: return a.copy()
    old=np.arange(len(a)); new=np.linspace(old[0],old[-1],n)
    return np.interp(new, old, a)
```

Compute ideal candidate:
```
def ideal_flat_candidate(n):
    L=2*n-1; M=fft_pow2(L)
    target=np.zeros(M); target[:L]=1.0/L
    T=np.fft.fft(target, M)
    # choose sqrt branch: np.sqrt(T) principal maybe.
    A=np.sqrt(T + 1e-300j)  # but principal
    a=np.fft.ifft(A, M).real[:n]
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    normalize
```

Detailed function implementations:

```
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import minimize
```
Need no evaluate_sequence import.

`fft_pow2(length)` as above.

`fast_conv(a)`:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    L = 2*n - 1
    M = fft_pow2(L)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    return np.fft.irfft(A*A, M)[:L]
```

`sequence_score(a)`:
```
def sequence_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.inf
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-12: return np.inf
    c = fast_conv(a)
    maxc = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(maxc): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*maxc/(S*S))
```

`clean_sequence(a, normalize=True)`:
```
def clean_sequence(a, normalize=True):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
       return np.empty(0)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-12:
       return np.zeros_like(a)
    if normalize:
       a = a/S
    return a
```
For fallback zeros, score inf.

`make_capped_target(c, reduction)` as above.
Need if c has length 1? n maybe 1? We can ignore, but handle: L=1, target. If reduction, M cap? capacity zero. Return None.

`spectral_refine` more details:
- Time budget maybe 0. If <=0 return.
- It prints. We'll pass `min_score` to stop if below? Could pass.
- It can accept `flat_target=None` to precompute? We can precompute per n? Not necessary but to reduce FFT of flat when fallback. We can have top-level `flat_target_fft(n, Mff=None)` cached? Closures not; can use global dict? Global mutable okay? But no closures. Simpler recompute; flat FFT each iteration when fallback only. For capped target, each target FFT.
```
def spectral_refine(a0, time_budget, reduce_init=5e-4, min_reduce=1e-8, etas=(1.0,0.6,0.3,0.15), verbose=True):
    a = clean_sequence(a0, normalize=True)
    if a.size < 2 or a.sum() < 1e-12:
        return a0
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + float(time_budget)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = sequence_score(a)
    reduce = reduce_init
    stall = 0
    iter_count=0
    n = len(a)
    L = 2*n -1
    Mff = fft_pow2(L)
    flat_target = np.zeros(L)
    flat_target[:] = 1.0 / L
    for iter_count in range(1000000):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       S = float(a.sum())
       if S <=1e-12: break
       c = fast_conv(a)
       c_norm = c/(S*S)
       target, Mc, C = make_capped_target(c_norm, reduce)
       if target is None:
           # if reduce too small, use flat occasionally
           if reduce < min_reduce * 1.1:
               target = flat_target
           else:
               reduce *= 0.5
               continue
       A = np.fft.fft(a, Mff)
       Cspec = A*A
       Tspec = np.fft.fft(target, Mff)
       accepted = False
       # Precompute valid mask
       valid = np.abs(A) > 1e-30
       for eta in etas:
           if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
           W = Cspec + eta * (Tspec - Cspec)
           A_new = A.copy()
           # Use np.where to avoid division by zero
           # For valid, A_new = 0.5*(A + W/A); invalid keep A
           # But doing A_new[valid] and boolean indexing might be ok.
           if np.any(valid):
               A_new[valid] = 0.5 * (A[valid] + W[valid] / A[valid])
           # If many invalid, no.
           a_new = np.fft.ifft(A_new, Mff).real[:n]
           a_new = np.maximum(a_new, 0.0)
           S_new = float(a_new.sum())
           if S_new < 1e-12:
               continue
           a_new = a_new/S_new
           sc = sequence_score(a_new)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
               a = a_new
               best_a = a.copy()
               best_sc = sc
               accepted = True
               if verbose:
                   print(f"    spectral t={time.monotonic()-t0:.1f}s eta={eta:.2f} red={reduce:.2e} score={sc:.8f}")
               break
       if accepted:
           stall = 0
           # reduce maybe lower if too small improvement? Keep. If best_sc improved a lot, can lower reduce to move faster? Maybe.
           # We can slightly reduce reduce to seek finer? Actually if high reduce accepted, keep to continue.
       else:
           stall += 1
           if stall >= 3:
               if reduce > min_reduce:
                   reduce *= 0.5
                   stall = 0
               else:
                   # try flat target with small etas? If already flat and no improve, break
                   if target is flat_target? Need flag.
                   break
    return best_a
```
Need avoid `if target is flat_target`; use flag `using_flat=False` when target assigned.

`lbfgs_refine` function:
```
def lbfgs_refine(a0, time_budget, beta=200, maxfun=3000, mu=1e4, verbose=True):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+time_budget
    a = clean_sequence(a0, normalize=True)
    x0 = a.copy().astype(np.float64)
    bounds=[(0.0, 1000.0)]*x0.size
    try:
       res = minimize(lse_objective, x0, args=(beta, mu), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds,
                      options={'ftol':1e-18, 'gtol':1e-12, 'maxiter':10000, 'maxfun':maxfun, 'maxls':50})
       a_out = np.clip(res.x,0,1000)
       if a_out.sum()<1e-12: a_out=a
       return a_out
    except Exception:
       return a
```
But `bounds` list length n = 7000; memory okay. For larger n 32768, list large but okay maybe. Could avoid bounds by clipping in objective? But bounds needed. Use list comprehension? Lambdas no, comprehension okay? "Don't use any lambda functions"; list comprehension is not lambda. Accept. But for n=32768, list of tuples memory ~2MB okay. If time, fine.

LSE function for objective should be top-level. Need `weighted_corr` efficient. Let's code:
```
def weighted_corr(P, a):
    n = a.size
    L = P.size
    # If lengths mismatch, pad/truncate P to 2n-1
    expected = 2*n - 1
    if L != expected:
       P2 = np.zeros(expected)
       m = min(L, expected)
       P2[:m]=P[:m]
       P = P2
    Q = P[::-1]
    M = fft_pow2(n + P.size)  # conv length n+L-1+? We need total length n+L-1. For next pow2 >= n+L-1. If use n+L maybe safe.
    conv = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a, M) * np.fft.rfft(Q, M), M)[:n + P.size - 1]
    return conv[P.size - 1 :: -1]
```
If M = fft_pow2(n + P.size - 1). We can pass length. Good.
Check `P.size` expected. Return length n.

Now candidate perturbation functions:
```
def smooth_perturb(a, rng, amp):
    n=a.size
    noise = rng.standard_normal(n)
    M=fft_pow2(n)
    Nf = np.fft.rfft(noise, M)
    freq = np.fft.rfftfreq(M)
    mask = np.where(freq < 0.2, 1.0, 0.0) # lowpass
    noise = np.fft.irfft(Nf*mask, M)[:n]
    # zero mean
    noise -= noise.mean()
    return a + amp*noise
```
But `np.where` array each call okay. Could add highpass too.

Symmetrize function:
```
def symmetrize_if_better(a):
    b=0.5*(a+a[::-1])
    b=clean_sequence(b, normalize=True)
    scb=sequence_score(b)
    sca=sequence_score(a)
    if scb<sca: return b, scb
    return a, sca
```

```
def resample_sequence(a, n):
    a = clean_sequence(a, normalize=True)
    old = np.arange(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    new = np.linspace(old[0], old[-1], n)
    b = np.interp(new, old, a)
    b = clean_sequence(b, normalize=True)
    return b
```

```
def build_fallback_candidates(n, rng):
    cands=[]
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    cands.append(np.ones(n))
    cands.append(np.maximum(0.5+0.4*np.cos(2*np.pi*x), 0.01))
    cands.append(np.maximum(0.3+0.7*x*(1-x),0.01))
    cands.append(np.maximum(0.5+0.3*np.cos(4*np.pi*x)+0.2*np.sin(2*np.pi*x),0.01))
    # ideal flat
    ideal = ideal_flat_candidate(n)
    if ideal.size==n and ideal.sum()>1e-12: cands.append(ideal)
    return [clean_sequence(a, normalize=True) for a in cands]
```

```
def ideal_flat_candidate(n):
    L=2*n-1; M=fft_pow2(L)
    target=np.zeros(M)
    target[:L]=1.0/L
    T=np.fft.fft(target, M)
    # Use iterative Newton from an initial A with positive DC to find branch that minimizes negative part?
    A = np.sqrt(T + 1e-300 + 0j)
    # Improve: repeatedly A = 0.5*(A + T/A), then truncate and make positive? That is just fixed point to sqrt(T) branch near initial.
    for _ in range(3):
       valid=np.abs(A)>1e-30
       Av=A[valid]; Tv=T[valid]
       corr=Tv/Av
       large=np.abs(corr)>5
       if np.any(large): corr[large]=corr[large]/(np.abs(corr[large])/5)
       A[valid]=0.5*(Av+corr)
    a=np.fft.ifft(A,M).real[:n]
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    if a.sum()>1e-12: a/=a.sum()
    return a
```

`smooth_perturb`:
```
def smooth_perturb(a, rng, amp):
   n=a.size; M=fft_pow2(n)
   noise=rng.standard_normal(n)
   Nf=np.fft.rfft(noise, M)
   freq=np.fft.rfftfreq(M)
   mask=(freq < 0.25).astype(np.float64)
   noise=np.fft.irfft(Nf*mask, M)[:n]
   noise -= noise.mean()
   return a + amp*noise
```
This creates mask each time; okay. For deterministic, rng.

KKT (D^TD+λI)δ = D^Tt. If λ>0 and D^T full row rank, solution δ=D^T(D D^T + λ I)^{-1}t. Yes. Good! This computes δ in row span of D (sum-zero not enforced). P dimension small. This is a powerful local linear step different from spectral. Let's implement `active_qp_refine` using this ridge step.

Algorithm:
```
def active_ridge_refine(a0, time_budget, reduce_init=2e-3, top_p=80, lam=1e-6, etas=(1.0,0.7,0.4,0.2), verbose=True):
 a=normalize
 best...
 acc=0; stall=0
 while time:
   c=fast_conv(a); M=max; C=M*(1-reduction)
   # active: indices where c > C (or top P if none)
   active = np.where(c > C + 1e-12)[0]
   if len(active)==0: reduction*=0.2? continue
   if len(active)>top_p: active = top P by c
   P=len(active)
   # build D rows
   D = np.zeros((P,n))
   for idx,k in enumerate(active):
      k=int(k)
      j_start=max(0,k-n+1); j_end=min(k,n-1)
      # D[idx, i] = a[k-i] for i=j_start..j_end? Wait δ_i variable a_i; D row for c_k: ∂c_k/∂a_i = a_{k-i}. Thus i range such that k-i in [0,n-1]. D[idx, i]=a[k-i].
      D[idx, j_start:j_end+1] = a[k - np.arange(j_start, j_end+1)]  # reversed slice
   # t = -0.5*(c[active]-C)
   t = -0.5*(c[active]-C)
   # Solve ridge: Dtd = D @ t; DAd = D @ D.T; A = DAd + lam*I
   # Need choose lam relative to D D^T scale. D D^T entries ~ sum a^2 ~1/n? Values ~1e-4. t~M*red~1e-7. lam maybe 1e-8? We can use lam = 1e-8 * max(diag) maybe.
   diag = np.diag(DAd); lam_eff = max(1e-12, 1e-6*diag.mean())
   A = DAd + lam_eff*np.eye(P)
   rhs = D @ t
   coeff = np.linalg.solve(A, rhs)
   delta = D.T @ coeff
   # Project zero sum: delta -= delta.mean()
   # Scale to bounds: find max scale s such that a + s*delta >=0 and <=1000? Since a sum1, delta small. Use nonneg only.
   neg = delta < 0; if any: max_s = min(a[i]/(-delta[i])) over neg; scale = min(1.0, 0.9*max_s) (leave margin)
   # Also maybe limit norm: if ||delta||>1e-3? scale by.
   delta *= scale
   # try etas: a_new = a + eta*delta; clip>=0; normalize; score; accept if improves
   for eta in etas: ...
   # if accept, stall=0; maybe slightly reduce reduction? keep
   else reduce*=0.5 if stall>3
```
But note: D row formula: c_k = Σ_i a_i a_{k-i}; ∂c_k/∂a_i = 2a_{k-i}. We set D idx,i = a_{k-i}. The vector of δ_i. Good. The active row D may have many entries but only ones in window. Because a may have small values. The ridge solution δ lies in span of D rows (each row is reversed segment). Since P small, δ has structure. It may not affect all coordinates. That's okay.
After adding δ and normalizing, linearized reduction approximate. Because D at current, if δ small, score improves.

This could be faster and targeted. It also breaks symmetry if multiple peaks; if one symmetric peak, D row is reversed a (for center), resulting δ proportional to a reversed; under sum, it pushes mass to make a more like? For two peaks symmetric, combination may reduce both. Good.

Let's test with n=2 symmetric a=[0.5,0.5], c=[.25,.5,.25], M=.5, red=.01 C=.495, active idx1 only. D row for k=1: i0 a1=0.5, i1 a0=0.5. D=[0.5,0.5]. DAd=[0.5], DAt=0.5*(-0.0025)=-0.00125. coeff=-0.00125/(0.5+lam)~-0.0025. δ=D.T coeff=[-0.00125,-0.00125], zero mean unchanged. This decreases both a, scaling after normalize no change. No improvement! Because reducing center peak by reducing both endpoints symmetrically doesn't break symmetry; linearization says any uniform scale reduces peak but sum normalization cancels. Need zero-sum direction to reduce peak: δ=[+ε,-ε] or [-ε,+ε] changes product. Our ridge solution gave δ in span D=[1,1], which is scale direction, projected zero mean zero. It doesn't find bracket direction. Need enforce zero-sum before solve: restrict δ to sum-zero subspace. We should solve for δ with sum δ=0 and minimize ||Dδ - t||^2 + λ||δ||^2. This is a quadratic in n variables with equality. We can eliminate mean: Let J=1/n 11^T. Any δ = (I-J)δ + mean. Since t reduction? The scale component D (mean direction) corresponds changing sum but after normalization no effect? Actually if δ has mean m1, under normalization a+δ scaled by 1/(1+m) approximately changes a by m*(1-a). It can affect product not purely scale.
```
"""
Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing 2n max(conv(a,a))/sum(a)^2.
Uses peak-capped spectral Newton updates on the flat-convolution target,
smoothed LSE BFGS refinement, smooth perturbations, and length resampling.
```

Detailed code snippets:

`fft_pow2`:
```
def fft_pow2(length):
    length = int(length)
    if length <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (length - 1).bit_length()
```

`fast_conv`: If a length 0? return empty? Not used.

`sequence_score`: Use `c = fast_conv(a)`, `maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0`. If maxc<0: maxc=0.

`clean_sequence`: If `normalize` and S>0, divide. If not normalize and S too small, zeros? For output, normalize true.

`make_capped_target`:
```
def make_capped_target(c, reduction):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    L = c.size
    if L == 0 or reduction <= 0:
        Mc = float(c.max()) if L else 0.0
        return None, Mc, 0.0
    cs = float(c.sum())
    if cs <= 1e-300:
        return None, 0.0, 0.0
    c = c / cs
    Mc = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(Mc) or Mc <= 0:
        return None, Mc, 0.0
    cap = Mc * (1.0 - reduction)
    if cap <= 0.0 or cap >= Mc:
        return None, Mc, 0.0
    t = np.minimum(c, cap)
    deficit = 1.0 - float(t.sum())
    if deficit <= 1e-14:
        return None, Mc, cap
    room = cap - t
    roomsum = float(room.sum())
    if roomsum <= 1e-15:
        return None, Mc, cap
    add = deficit / roomsum
    if add >= 1.0:
        return None, Mc, cap
    target = t + add * room
    ts = float(target.sum())
    if ts <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(ts):
        return None, Mc, cap
    target = target / ts
    # In case numerical overshoot, softly cap? 
    if float(target.max()) > cap * (1.0 + 1e-9) + 1e-15:
        target = np.minimum(target, cap)
        ts = float(target.sum())
        if ts > 0: target = target / ts
    return target, Mc, cap
```
If after cap we renormalize, max could exceed cap if ts<1. But add<1 ensures sum1; if we accidentally min, ts may <1 and normalization raises max. Better not min unless tiny. So avoid min. Just return.

`spectral_refine`:
Need parameters maybe `max_iters` none. Use `last_print = 0.0`.
```
def spectral_refine(a0, time_budget, reduce_init=2e-3, min_reduce=1e-7, etas=(1.0,0.6,0.3,0.15), verbose=False):
    a = clean_sequence(a0, normalize=True)
    if a.size < 2 or float(a.sum()) < 1e-12:
        return np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(0.0,float(time_budget))
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1; Mff=fft_pow2(L)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sequence_score(a)
    reduce=reduce_init; stall=0; using_flat=False
    flat_target = np.ones(L) / L
    flat_fft = np.fft.fft(flat_target, Mff)
    iter_no=0; last_print=t0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if time.monotonic() - last_print > 10 and verbose:
           print(f"  spectral refine it={iter_no} t={time.monotonic()-t0:.0f}s score={best_sc:.8f} reduce={reduce:.2e}")
           last_print=time.monotonic()
       S=float(a.sum())
       if S<1e-12: break
       c=fast_conv(a)
       c_norm=c/(S*S)
       target, Mc, cap = make_capped_target(c_norm, reduce)
       using_flat=False
       if target is None:
           if reduce < min_reduce * 1.01:
               target=flat_target
               Tspec=flat_fft
               using_flat=True
           else:
               reduce *= 0.5
               continue
       else:
           Tspec=np.fft.fft(target, Mff)
       A=np.fft.fft(a, Mff)
       Cspec=A*A
       valid=np.abs(A)>1e-30
       accepted=False
       for eta in etas:
           if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
           W=Cspec + eta*(Tspec-Cspec)
           A_new=A.copy()
           if np.any(valid):
               Av=A[valid]
               Wv=W[valid]
               corr=Wv/Av
               lim=5.0
               large=np.abs(corr)>lim
               if np.any(large):
                   corr[large] = corr[large] / (np.abs(corr[large]) / lim)
               A_new[valid] = 0.5*(Av + corr)
           a_new=np.fft.ifft(A_new, Mff).real[:n]
           a_new=np.maximum(a_new,0.0)
           S_new=float(a_new.sum())
           if S_new < 1e-12: continue
           a_new /= S_new
           sc=sequence_score(a_new)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
               a=a_new; best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=sc; accepted=True
               if verbose:
                   print(f"    spectral accept t={time.monotonic()-t0:.0f}s eta={eta:.2f} red={reduce:.2e} score={best_sc:.8f}")
               last_print=time.monotonic()
               break
       iter_no +=1
       if accepted:
           stall=0
           # if close to lower bound, make reduction smaller to avoid over
           floor = 2.0*n/(2.0*n-1.0)
           if best_sc > floor:
              gap = (best_sc-floor)/(2*n)?? not needed
           # Keep reduce; maybe if using flat and accepted, reduce capped? not.
       else:
           stall +=1
           if stall >= 3:
              if not using_flat:
                  reduce *=0.5
                  stall=0
              else:
                  break
    return best_a
```
The `gap` code remove.

Potential issue: `flat_fft` computed every spectral_refine call. Good.

If `target is None` and reduce below min, we use flat. But if using flat, make_capped_target not called? We set target flat and Tspec. Good.

Could if using flat but `flat_fft` zero? fine.

`weighted_corr`: ensure if M smaller than needed? Use `total = n + P.size - 1`, `M=fft_pow2(total)`. `conv = np.fft.irfft(... )[:total]`. Return slice length n. For P length expected. Use float.

`lse_objective`: Need use top-level weighted_corr. If `beta` very high, exps underflow; use `scaled = beta*(c_norm-cmax)`. Since max zero, min maybe -1e5, exp underflow to 0. Use `np.exp(scaled, out?)` no. Underflow okay. Sum at least 1 (max term exp0). `d` finite.
```
def lse_objective(x, beta, mu=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-12:
        return 1e20, np.ones(n)  # penalty gradient? if S<1, grad mu(-1) huge? Use zeros? Better np.ones for penalty.
    c=fast_conv(a)
    S2=S*S
    m=c/S2
    Lint=m.size
    mmax=float(m.max())
    scaled=beta*(m-mmax)
    # protect underflow
    expv=np.exp(scaled)
    d=float(expv.sum())
    if d <=0 or not np.isfinite(d): return 1e20, np.ones(n)
    R=mmax + float(np.log(d))/beta
    w=expv/d
    b=weighted_corr(w, a)
    h=2.0*b
    val=R
    grad=h/S2 - (2.0*R/S)*np.ones(n)
    if mu != 0.0:
       pen=0.5*mu*(S-1.0)**2
       val += pen
       grad += mu*(S-1.0)*np.ones(n)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
       val=1e20; grad=np.ones(n)
    return val, grad
```
If S<=1e-12, return large and gradient `ones`? Penalty gradient should be -mu. Use `grad = -np.ones(n)*mu` maybe. But L-BFGS may fix. Use `np.ones(n)`.

`mu` default 1.0. If `mu` very large, okay.

`lbfgs_refine`:
```
def lbfgs_refine(a0, time_budget, beta=2000, maxfun=1500, mu=1.0, verbose=False):
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(0.0,float(time_budget))
    a=clean_sequence(a0, normalize=True)
    if a.size<2: return a
    x0=a.astype(np.float64).copy()
    bounds=[(0.0, 1000.0)]*x0.size
    try:
       res = minimize(lse_objective, x0, args=(beta, mu), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds,
                      options={'ftol':1e-18, 'gtol':1e-12, 'maxiter':500, 'maxfun':int(maxfun), 'maxls':30})
       out=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
       out=a
    out=np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out=np.clip(out,0.0,1000.0)
    S=float(out.sum())
    if S<1e-12: return a
    return out/S
```
No time check inside minimize; if maxfun too high may overrun. Keep maxfun limited. Could pass `maxiter=200` for large n. Use `maxiter = max(50, 20000000 // len(a0) // 10?)`. Simpler `maxiter=300`. For n=7k, 300 iterations and maxfun1500 okay. Use.

`smooth_perturb` and `generate_perturbations`:
```
def smooth_perturb(a, rng, amp):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return a
    M=fft_pow2(n)
    noise = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(n), M), M)[:n]
    freq=np.fft.rfftfreq(M)
    mask=(freq < 0.25).astype(np.float64)
    noise=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(noise, M)*mask, M)[:n]
    noise -= float(noise.mean())
    return a + amp*noise
```
This does two FFTs; okay. Could combine: generate noise, FFT, multiply mask, inverse. Use one:
```
noise_freq = np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(n), M); noise_freq *= mask; noise=irfft(...)
```
Good.

`generate_perturbations(a, rng, count=4)`:
```
def generate_perturbations(a, rng, count=4):
    out=[]
    for amp in (0.01,0.03,0.08,0.2):
       b=clean_sequence(smooth_perturb(a,rng,amp), normalize=True)
       if b.size==a.size and float(b.sum())>1e-12: out.append(b)
       if len(out)>=count: break
    # log perturb
    for amp in (0.05,0.2):
       if len(out)>=count: break
       base=np.maximum(a,1e-15)
       loga=np.log(base)
       noise=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(a.size))*mask, ...) # need mask. Could call smooth_perturb on log? no.
       loga += amp*noise
       b=np.exp(loga - np.max(loga)); b/=b.sum(); clean; append
    return out
```
Need mask function to avoid recomputing. Define `lowpass_mask(n, cutoff=0.25)` top-level returning M and mask.
```
def lowpass_mask(n, cutoff=0.25):
    M=fft_pow2(n)
    f=np.fft.rfftfreq(M)
    return M, (f < cutoff).astype(np.float64)
```
Then `smooth_perturb` uses it. For log perturb, use same mask.

`resample_sequence`:
```
def resample_sequence(a, n):
    a=clean_sequence(a, normalize=True)
    if a.size==n: return a.copy()
    old=np.arange(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    new=np.linspace(0.0, float(a.size-1), n)
    b=np.interp(new, old, a)
    return clean_sequence(b, normalize=True)
```
If a.size=0? no.

`symmetrize_if_better`:
```
def symmetrize_if_better(a):
    a=clean_sequence(a, normalize=True)
    sc_a=sequence_score(a)
    b=0.5*(a+a[::-1])
    b=clean_sequence(b, normalize=True)
    sc_b=sequence_score(b)
    if np.isfinite(sc_b) and sc_b < sc_a:
       return b, sc_b
    return a, sc_a
```

```
def ideal_flat_candidate(n):
    L=2*n-1; M=fft_pow2(L)
    target=np.zeros(M); target[:L]=1.0/L
    T=np.fft.fft(target,M)
    A=np.sqrt(T + 1e-300 + 0j)
    # choose phase to maximize positive mass in first n
    best=None; best_sc=np.inf
    for phase in (0.0, np.pi/2, -np.pi/2, np.pi, np.pi/4, 3*np.pi/4, -np.pi/4, -3*np.pi/4):
       A_phi=A*np.exp(1j*phase)
       a=np.fft.ifft(A_phi,M).real[:n]
       a=np.maximum(a,0.0); S=float(a.sum())
       if S>1e-12:
          a/=S
          sc=sequence_score(a)
          if sc<best_sc:
             best_sc=sc; best=a.copy()
    if best is None: best=np.ones(n)
    return best
```

Double-check for variables:

- In `weighted_corr`, if P size not expected, we adjust. Need `P = np.asarray(P, dtype=np.float64).ravel()`.
```
def weighted_corr(P, a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel(); P=np.asarray(P,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size; expected=2*n-1
    if P.size != expected:
       P2=np.zeros(expected); m=min(P.size,expected); P2[:m]=P[:m]; P=P2
    Q=P[::-1]
    total=n+P.size-1
    M=fft_pow2(total)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a,M)*np.fft.rfft(Q,M), M)[:total]
    # slice length n
    return conv[P.size-1 : P.size-n-1 : -1]
```
If `P.size-n-1` equals -1? P=2n-1 => P-n-1 = n-2? For n=2, 3-2-1=0, slice 2:0:-1 len2. For n=1, P=1, stop=1-1-1=-1, slice 0:-1:-1 returns empty? Let's test n=1: unique. Need handle n=1 return P[0]*a? But sequences length >1 likely. To be safe:
```
if n == 1: return np.array([float(P[0]*a[0])])? Actually b_i = P0*a0? For L=1.
start=P.size-1
stop=P.size-n-1
if stop < 0: stop = -1
return conv[start:stop:-1][:n]
```
For n=1, start0, stop=-1, arr[0:-1:-1] empty? In numpy, slice 0:-1:-1 with stop -1 means stop index len-1? For negative step semantics tricky. Simpler handle n==1. But n at least maybe 64. Ignore? Better:
```
slice_arr = conv[P.size-1 :: -1]
return slice_arr[:n]
```
For n=2, conv[2::-1] length3, [:2]=[2,1] correct. For n=3, conv[4::-1] length5, [:3]=[4,3,2] correct. So use this simpler.
```
return conv[P.size-1::-1][:n]
```
If P.size=1,n=1 returns conv[0::-1] length1. Good.

In `fast_conv`, `M=fft_pow2(L)`. If n=1 L=1 M=1, rfft length1 okay, irfft length1. Good.

Now, `ideal_flat_candidate`: `target=np.zeros(M); target[:L]=1.0/L` but M>=L. T=fft(target,M). A=sqrt. For phase loop, global phase multiplying A: if A not conjugate symmetric? principal sqrt of T (T conjugate symmetric) yields A conjugate symmetric? For negative real T[k], sqrt gives i*sqrt; T[M-k] conjugate? If T[k] negative real, T[M-k] also negative real, sqrt gives -i? Principal sqrt of negative real + i0 is +i*sqrt, for conjugate same negative, gives +i, not conjugate (-i). This breaks realness. However T[k] may have tiny imaginary phase; principal branch conjugate symmetry may choose different branches? For real even T, if T[k] negative with phase π, principal sqrt phase π/2; for M-k also phase π (if stored as exact) -> sqrt phase π/2, not conjugate. Inverse may have imaginary. We take real. Global phases not fix branch. Our GS/parent avoids by branch aligned to A. Ideal candidate may be poor. We can choose branch to enforce conjugate symmetry: after computing A, for k=1..M/2-1 set A[M-k] = conj(A[k]), and A[0],A[M/2] real. This picks principal for first half. It may reduce realness. Implement? Not necessary if only fallback.
```

def smooth_perturb(a, rng, amp):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return a.copy()
    M, mask = lowpass_mask(n, 0.25)
    noise=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(n), M)*mask, M)[:n]
    noise -= float(noise.mean())
    return a + amp*noise
```

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a))^2.

The main ideas are:
1. Peak-capped spectral Newton: work in the Fourier domain where
   conv(a,a) corresponds to A(z)^2.  From the current convolution, build a
   new non-negative target whose peaks are capped; move A toward sqrt(target
   spectrum) with line search and non-negativity truncation.
2. Smooth LSE BFGS refinement on the normalized convolution using a log-sum-exp
   smoothing of the maximum coefficient, with a small penalty to keep sum(a) near 1.
3. Smooth/exponential perturbations and optional length resampling to avoid local minima.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
except Exception:
    minimize = None


def fft_pow2(length):
    length = int(length)
    if length <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (length - 1).bit_length()


def fast_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    M = fft_pow2(L)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    return np.fft.irfft(A * A, M)[:L]


def sequence_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    S = float(np.sum(a))
    if S < 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    c = fast_conv(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    maxc = float(np.max(c))
    if maxc < 0.0:
        maxc = 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(maxc):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * maxc / (S * S))


def clean_sequence(a, normalize=True):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(np.sum(a))
    if S <= 1e-12:
        return np.zeros(a.shape, dtype=np.float64)
    if normalize:
        a = a / S
    return a


def make_capped_target(c, reduction):
    """
    Given a normalized convolution c (sum(c)=1), cap coefficients at
    max(c)*(1-reduction) and redistribute the missing mass proportionally to
    capacity below the cap.  Returns (target, max_c, cap), or None target.
    """
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    L = c.size
    if L == 0 or reduction <= 0.0:
        mc = float(np.max(c)) if L else 0.0
        return None, mc, 0.0

    cs = float(np.sum(c))
    if cs <= 1e-300:
        return None, 0.0, 0.0
    c = c / cs

    mc = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mc) or mc <= 0.0:
        return None, mc, 0.0

    cap = mc * (1.0 - reduction)
    if cap <= 0.0 or cap >= mc:
        return None, mc, 0.0

    t = np.minimum(c, cap)
    deficit = 1.0 - float(np.sum(t))
    if deficit <= 1e-14:
        return None, mc, cap

    room = cap - t
    roomsum = float(np.sum(room))
    if roomsum <= 1e-15:
        return None, mc, cap

    add = deficit / roomsum
    if add >= 1.0:
        # Cap is too low to maintain total mass 1 while keeping all entries <= cap.
        return None, mc, cap

    target = t + add * room
    ts = float(np.sum(target))
    if ts <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(ts):
        return None, mc, cap

    target = target / ts
    return target, mc, cap


def spectral_refine(a0, time_budget, reduce_init=2e-3, min_reduce=1e-7,
                    etas=(1.0, 0.6, 0.3, 0.15), verbose=False):
    """
    Spectral peak-capping Newton iterations.

    For current a, form a target convolution c_target with capped peaks.  In
    frequency, move A toward sqrt(FFT(c_target)) by the Newton/square-root
    update A <- 0.5*(A + W/A), line-search over eta, truncate to length n,
    project non-negative, renormalize, and accept only exact score improvements.
    """
    a = clean_sequence(a0, normalize=True)
    if a.size < 2 or float(np.sum(a)) < 1e-12:
        return np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(time_budget))

    n = a.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    M = fft_pow2(L)

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = sequence_score(a)

    reduce = reduce_init
    stall = 0
    using_flat = False
    flat_target = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
    flat_fft = np.fft.fft(flat_target, M)

    iter_no = 0
    last_print = t0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if verbose and (time.monotonic() - last_print > 10.0):
            print(f"    spectral t={time.monotonic()-t0:.0f}s it={iter_no} red={reduce:.2e} score={best_sc:.8f}")
            last_print = time.monotonic()

        S = float(np.sum(a))
        if S < 1e-12:
            break

        c = fast_conv(a)
        c_norm = c / (S * S)

        target, mc, cap = make_capped_target(c_norm, reduce)
        using_flat = False

        if target is None:
            if reduce < min_reduce * 1.01:
                target = flat_target
                T = flat_fft
                using_flat = True
            else:
                reduce *= 0.5
                continue
        else:
            T = np.fft.fft(target, M)

        A = np.fft.fft(a, M)
        C = A * A
        valid = np.abs(A) > 1e-30

        accepted = False
        for eta in etas:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break

            W = C + eta * (T - C)
            A_new = A.copy()

            if np.any(valid):
                Av = A[valid]
                Wv = W[valid]
                corr = Wv / Av
                lim = 5.0
                large = np.abs(corr) > lim
                if np.any(large):
                    corr[large] = corr[large] / (np.abs(corr[large]) / lim)
                A_new[valid] = 0.5 * (Av + corr)

            a_new = np.fft.ifft(A_new, M).real[:n]
            a_new = np.maximum(a_new, 0.0)
            S_new = float(np.sum(a_new))
            if S_new < 1e-12:
                continue
            a_new /= S_new

            sc = sequence_score(a_new)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
                a = a_new
                best_a = a.copy()
                best_sc = sc
                accepted = True
                if verbose:
                    print(f"    spectral accept t={time.monotonic()-t0:.0f}s eta={eta:.2f} red={reduce:.2e} score={best_sc:.8f}")
                    last_print = time.monotonic()
                break

        iter_no += 1

        if accepted:
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall >= 3:
                if not using_flat:
                    reduce *= 0.5
                    stall = 0
                else:
                    break

    return best_a


def weighted_corr(P, a):
    """
    Compute b_i = sum_j P[i+j] a_j for i=0..n-1, where P has length 2n-1.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    P = np.asarray(P, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    expected = 2 * n - 1
    if P.size != expected:
        P2 = np.zeros(expected, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(P.size, expected)
        P2[:m] = P[:m]
        P = P2

    Q = P[::-1]
    total = n + P.size - 1
    M = fft_pow2(total)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a, M) * np.fft.rfft(Q, M), M)[:total]
    return conv[P.size - 1::-1][:n]


def lse_objective(x, beta, mu=1.0):
    """
    Smooth objective: LSE of normalized convolution plus small sum penalty.
    Returns (value, gradient).
    """
    a = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return 1e20, np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    S = float(np.sum(a))
    if S <= 1e-12:
        return 1e20, -mu * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    c = fast_conv(a)
    S2 = S * S
    m = c / S2
    mmax = float(np.max(m))

    scaled = beta * (m - mmax)
    expv = np.exp(scaled)
    d = float(np.sum(expv))
    if d <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(d):
        return 1e20, -mu * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    R = mmax + float(np.log(d)) / beta
    w = expv / d

    b = weighted_corr(w, a)
    h = 2.0 * b

    val = R
    grad = h / S2 - (2.0 * R / S) * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if mu != 0.0:
        pen = 0.5 * mu * (S - 1.0) ** 2
        val += pen
        grad += mu * (S - 1.0) * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
        return 1e20, -mu * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    return val, grad


def lbfgs_refine(a0, time_budget, beta=2000, maxfun=1500, mu=1.0, verbose=False):
    """Run a short L-BFGS-B refinement on the smooth LSE objective."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(time_budget))

    a = clean_sequence(a0, normalize=True)
    if a.size < 2 or float(np.sum(a)) < 1e-12 or minimize is None:
        return a

    x0 = a.astype(np.float64).copy()
    bounds = [(0.0, 1000.0)] * x0.size

    # Keep runtime bounded even if maxfun is large.
    maxiter = max(50, min(350, int(25000000.0 / max(1, x0.size))))

    try:
        res = minimize(
            lse_objective,
            x0,
            args=(beta, mu),
            method='L-BFGS-B',
            jac=True,
            bounds=bounds,
            options={'ftol': 1e-18, 'gtol': 1e-12,
                     'maxiter': int(maxiter),
                     'maxfun': int(maxfun),
                     'maxls': 30}
        )
        out = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        out = a

    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(np.sum(out))
    if S < 1e-12:
        return a
    return out / S


def lowpass_mask(n, cutoff=0.25):
    M = fft_pow2(n)
    freq = np.fft.rfftfreq(M)
    mask = (freq < cutoff).astype(np.float64)
    return M, mask


def smooth_perturb(a, rng, amp):
    a = clean_sequence(a, normalize=True)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a
    M, mask = lowpass_mask(n, 0.25)
    noise = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(n), M) * mask, M)[:n]
    noise -= float(np.mean(noise))
    return a + amp * noise


def generate_perturbations(a, rng, count=4):
    a = clean_sequence(a, normalize=True)
    out = []

    for amp in (0.01, 0.03, 0.08, 0.20):
        if len(out) >= count:
            break
        b = clean_sequence(smooth_perturb(a, rng, amp), normalize=True)
        if b.size == a.size and float(np.sum(b)) > 1e-12:
            out.append(b)

    n = a.size
    if n > 0:
        M, mask = lowpass_mask(n, 0.25)
        for amp in (0.05, 0.20, 0.50):
            if len(out) >= count:
                break
            base = np.maximum(a, 1e-15)
            loga = np.log(base)
            noise = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(n), M) * mask, M)[:n]
            noise -= float(np.mean(noise))
            loga = loga + amp * noise
            b = np.exp(loga - float(np.max(loga)))
            S = float(np.sum(b))
            if S > 1e-12:
                b = b / S
                b = clean_sequence(b, normalize=True)
                if b.size == a.size:
                    out.append(b)

    return out[:count]


def resample_sequence(a, n):
    a = clean_sequence(a, normalize=True)
    if a.size == n:
        return a.copy()
    if a.size == 0 or n <= 0:
        return a
    old = np.arange(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    new = np.linspace(0.0, float(a.size - 1), n)
    b = np.interp(new, old, a)
    return clean_sequence(b, normalize=True)


def symmetrize_if_better(a):
    a = clean_sequence(a, normalize=True)
    sc_a = sequence_score(a)
    if a.size <= 1 or not np.isfinite(sc_a):
        return a, sc_a

    b = 0.5 * (a + a[::-1])
    b = clean_sequence(b, normalize=True)
    sc_b = sequence_score(b)
    if np.isfinite(sc_b) and sc_b < sc_a - 1e-13:
        return b, sc_b
    return a, sc_a


def ideal_flat_candidate(n):
    """
    A rough spectral square-root initializer for the flat convolution target.
    Usually not alone optimal, but useful as a diverse starting point.
    """
    L = 2 * n - 1
    M = fft_pow2(L)
    target = np.zeros(M, dtype=np.float64)
    target[:L] = 1.0 / float(L)
    T = np.fft.fft(target, M)

    A = np.sqrt(T + 1e-300 + 0j)
    # Enforce conjugate symmetry as much as possible.
    for k in range(1, M // 2 + 1):
        if k == M - k:
            continue
        A[M - k] = np.conj(A[k])
    if M % 2 == 0:
        A[M // 2] = float(np.real(A[M // 2]))
    A[0] = float(np.real(A[0]))

    best = None
    best_sc = np.inf
    phases = (0.0, np.pi / 2.0, -np.pi / 2.0, np.pi,
              np.pi / 4.0, 3.0 * np.pi / 4.0, -np.pi / 4.0, -3.0 * np.pi / 4.0)
    for ph in phases:
        aph = A * np.exp(1j * ph)
        a = np.fft.ifft(aph, M).real[:n]
        a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
        S = float(np.sum(a))
        if S > 1e-12:
            a = a / S
            sc = sequence_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best = a.copy()

    if best is None:
        best = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    return clean_sequence(best, normalize=True)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    total = float(budget_s)
    reserve_s = 60.0
    available = max(60.0, total - reserve_s)
    deadline = t_start + available

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Load parent sequence if present.
    parent = None
    try:
        hv = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
        if hv is not None:
            p = clean_sequence(hv, normalize=True)
            if p.size >= 8 and float(np.sum(p)) > 1e-12:
                parent = p
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is None:
        n0 = 6962
        try:
            parent = ideal_flat_candidate(n0)
        except Exception:
            parent = np.ones(n0, dtype=np.float64) * 0.5
        parent = clean_sequence(parent, normalize=True)

    best_a, best_sc = symmetrize_if_better(parent)
    print(f"Initial: n={len(best_a)} score={best_sc:.8f}")

    # If the parent is unusable, try a few basic starts.
    if not np.isfinite(best_sc) or best_sc > 2.0:
        n0 = 6962
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n0)
        fallbacks = [
            np.ones(n0, dtype=np.float64) * 0.5,
            np.maximum(0.5 + 0.4 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x), 0.01),
            np.maximum(0.3 + 0.7 * x * (1.0 - x), 0.01),
            np.maximum(0.5 + 0.3 * np.cos(4.0 * np.pi * x) + 0.2 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x), 0.01),
        ]
        for fb in fallbacks:
            cand = clean_sequence(fb, normalize=True)
            sc = sequence_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_a = cand
                best_sc = sc
                print(f"  fallback improved: {sc:.8f}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 1: strong spectral peak-capping refinement.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    now = time.monotonic()
    t1 = min(0.40 * available, deadline - now - 30.0)
    if t1 > 30.0:
        print(f"\nPhase 1: spectral peak-capping, budget={t1:.0f}s")
        cand = spectral_refine(
            best_a, t1,
            reduce_init=2e-3,
            min_reduce=1e-7,
            etas=(1.0, 0.6, 0.3, 0.15),
            verbose=True
        )
        cand = clean_sequence(cand, normalize=True)
        sc = sequence_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
            best_a = cand
            best_sc = sc
            print(f"  Phase 1 best: {best_sc:.8f}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 2: LSE L-BFGS polishing with several smoothing scales.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    now = time.monotonic()
    t2 = min(0.20 * available, deadline - now - 70.0)
    if t2 > 60.0:
        print(f"\nPhase 2: LSE L-BFGS, budget={t2:.0f}s")
        beta_list = (2000, 10000, 50000)
        for idx, beta in enumerate(beta_list):
            now = time.monotonic()
            rem = deadline - now
            if rem < 80.0:
                break
            remaining_runs = len(beta_list) - idx
            t_run = min(180.0, rem / float(max(1, remaining_runs)))
            if t_run < 30.0:
                break

            base_maxfun = max(400, min(3000, int(20000000.0 / max(1, len(best_a)))))
            mf = int(max(400, min(3000, base_maxfun * max(0.2, min(1.0, t_run / 180.0)))))

            a_lb = lbfgs_refine(best_a, t_run, beta=beta, maxfun=mf, mu=1.0)
            cand = clean_sequence(a_lb, normalize=True)
            sc = sequence_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
                best_a = cand
                best_sc = sc
                print(f"  LBFGS beta={beta}: {best_sc:.8f} in {t_run:.1f}s")
            else:
                print(f"  LBFGS beta={beta}: no improvement (best={best_sc:.8f})")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 3: another spectral pass, usually with smaller cap reductions.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    now = time.monotonic()
    t3 = min(0.15 * available, deadline - now - 50.0)
    if t3 > 50.0:
        print(f"\nPhase 3: spectral polish, budget={t3:.0f}s")
        cand = spectral_refine(
            best_a, t3,
            reduce_init=5e-4,
            min_reduce=1e-8,
            etas=(1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.10),
            verbose=False
        )
        cand = clean_sequence(cand, normalize=True)
        sc = sequence_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
            best_a = cand
            best_sc = sc
            print(f"  Phase 3 best: {best_sc:.8f}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 4: smooth perturbations around the current best.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    now = time.monotonic()
    t4 = min(0.12 * available, deadline - now - 120.0)
    if t4 > 60.0:
        print(f"\nPhase 4: perturbations, budget={t4:.0f}s")
        cands = generate_perturbations(best_a, rng, count=4)
        for idx, cand0 in enumerate(cands):
            now = time.monotonic()
            rem = deadline - now
            if rem < 70.0:
                break
            remaining_runs = max(1, len(cands) - idx)
            t_p = min(80.0, rem / float(remaining_runs))
            if t_p < 30.0:
                break

            cand = spectral_refine(
                cand0, t_p,
                reduce_init=1e-3,
                min_reduce=1e-7,
                etas=(1.0, 0.5, 0.25),
                verbose=False
            )
            cand = clean_sequence(cand, normalize=True)
            sc = sequence_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
                best_a = cand
                best_sc = sc
                print(f"  perturbation {idx}: {best_sc:.8f}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 5: try a few longer sequences by resampling the best one.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    now = time.monotonic()
    t5 = min(0.13 * available, deadline - now - 60.0)
    if t5 > 60.0:
        print(f"\nPhase 5: resampling, budget={t5:.0f}s")
        bn = len(best_a)
        opts = []
        for n2 in (8192, 16384, 24576, 32768):
            if n2 > bn and n2 <= 2 * bn + 5000 and n2 <= 32768:
                opts.append(n2)
        if not opts and bn < 16384:
            opts.append(16384)
        opts = opts[:2]

        for idx, n2 in enumerate(opts):
            now = time.monotonic()
            rem = deadline - now
            if rem < 100.0:
                break
            remaining_runs = max(1, len(opts) - idx)
            t_u = min(120.0, rem / float(remaining_runs))
            if t_u < 40.0:
                break

            a2 = resample_sequence(best_a, n2)
            a2 = spectral_refine(
                a2, t_u,
                reduce_init=2e-3,
                min_reduce=1e-7,
                etas=(1.0, 0.5, 0.25),
                verbose=False
            )
            a2 = clean_sequence(a2, normalize=True)
            sc = sequence_score(a2)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
                best_a = a2
                best_sc = sc
                print(f"  resample n={n2}: {best_sc:.8f}")

    # Final symmetrization check.
    best_a, best_sc = symmetrize_if_better(best_a)

    out_array = clean_sequence(best_a, normalize=True)
    if out_array.size == 0 or float(np.sum(out_array)) < 0.01:
        out_array = np.ones(6962, dtype=np.float64) * 0.5

    out = [max(0.0, min(1000.0, float(v))) for v in out_array.tolist()]
    if sum(out) < 0.01:
        out = [0.5] * 6962

    verified = best_sc
    # Only call evaluate_sequence for moderate lengths where direct convolve is safe
    # within the reserved time.
    if len(out) <= 12000 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        try:
            verified = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
        except Exception:
            verified = best_sc

    elapsed = time.monotonic() - t_start
    print(f"\nFINAL: internal={best_sc:.8f}, verified={verified:.8f}, n={len(out)}, time={elapsed:.1f}s")
    return out
```
